</section><section data-mw-section-id="37" id="mwB2A" aria-labelledby="CSS_4"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="CSS_4">CSS 4</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: CSS 4"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt402" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwB2E" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;redirect&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Redirect&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;CSS4&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt403" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt402" id="mwB2I">"CSS4" redirects here. For other uses, see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSS4_(disambiguation)" title="CSS4 (disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig">CSS4 (disambiguation)</a>.</div>

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwB2M"><span id="mwB2Q"><video poster="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/CSS_Standardization_-_The_State_of_the_Web.webm/250px--CSS_Standardization_-_The_State_of_the_Web.webm.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser" controls="" preload="none" data-mw-tmh="" height="141" width="250" resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:CSS_Standardization_-_The_State_of_the_Web.webm" data-durationhint="1279" class="mw-file-element" id="mwB2U"><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/22/CSS_Standardization_-_The_State_of_the_Web.webm?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=index&amp;utm_content=original" type="video/webm; codecs=&quot;vp9, opus&quot;" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="720" id="mwB2Y"><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/2/22/CSS_Standardization_-_The_State_of_the_Web.webm/CSS_Standardization_-_The_State_of_the_Web.webm.240p.vp9.webm" type="video/webm; codecs=&quot;vp9, opus&quot;" data-width="426" data-height="240" data-transcodekey="240p.vp9.webm" id="mwB2c"><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/2/22/CSS_Standardization_-_The_State_of_the_Web.webm/CSS_Standardization_-_The_State_of_the_Web.webm.360p.mpeg4.mov" type="video/quicktime" data-width="640" data-height="360" data-transcodekey="360p.mpeg4.mov" id="mwB2g"><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/2/22/CSS_Standardization_-_The_State_of_the_Web.webm/CSS_Standardization_-_The_State_of_the_Web.webm.480p.vp9.webm" type="video/webm; codecs=&quot;vp9, opus&quot;" data-width="854" data-height="480" data-transcodekey="480p.vp9.webm" id="mwB2k"></video></span><figcaption id="mwB2o"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jen_Simmons" title="Jen Simmons" id="mwB2s">Jen Simmons</a> discussing the state of CSS in 2019, as several CSS<span class="nowrap" about="#mwt404" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwB2w" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;nbsp&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Nbsp&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><span typeof="mw:Entity">&nbsp;</span></span>4 modules were being advanced</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwB20">There is no CSS4 specification,<sup about="#mwt407" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-55" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-55&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-55" id="mwB24"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB28"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB3A">[</span>55<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB3E">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt410" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-56" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-56&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-56" id="mwB3I"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB3M"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB3Q">[</span>56<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB3U">]</span></span></a></sup> because CSS has been split into many separate modules which level independently.</p>

<p id="mwB3Y">Modules that build on things from CSS Level 2 started at Level 3. Some of them have already reached Level 4 or are already approaching Level 5. Other modules that define entirely new functionality, such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flexbox" title="Flexbox" id="mwB3c">Flexbox</a>,<sup about="#mwt413" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-57" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-57&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-57" id="mwB3g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB3k"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB3o">[</span>57<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB3s">]</span></span></a></sup> have been designated as Level 1 and some of them are approaching Level 2.</p>

<p id="mwB3w">The CSS Working Group sometimes publishes "Snapshots", a collection of whole modules and parts of other drafts that are considered stable enough to be implemented by browser developers. So far, five such "best current practices" documents have been published as Notes, in 2007,<sup about="#mwt416" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-58" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-58&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-58" id="mwB30"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB34"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB38">[</span>58<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB4A">]</span></span></a></sup> 2010,<sup about="#mwt419" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-59" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-59&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-59" id="mwB4E"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB4I"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB4M">[</span>59<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB4Q">]</span></span></a></sup> 2015,<sup about="#mwt422" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-60" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-60&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-60" id="mwB4U"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB4Y"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB4c">[</span>60<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB4g">]</span></span></a></sup> 2017,<sup about="#mwt425" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-61" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-61&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-61" id="mwB4k"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB4o"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB4s">[</span>61<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB4w">]</span></span></a></sup> and 2018.<sup about="#mwt428" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-62" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-62&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-62" id="mwB40"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB44"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB48">[</span>62<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB5A">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwB5E">Since these specification snapshots are primarily intended for developers, there has been a growing demand for a similar versioned reference document targeted at authors, which would present the state of interoperable implementations as meanwhile documented by sites like Can I Use...<sup about="#mwt431" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-caniuse_63-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;caniuse&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-caniuse-63&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-caniuse-63" id="mwB5I"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB5M"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB5Q">[</span>63<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB5U">]</span></span></a></sup> and the MDN Web Docs.<sup about="#mwt434" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-mdn_64-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;mdn&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-mdn-64&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-mdn-64" id="mwB5Y"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB5c"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB5g">[</span>64<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB5k">]</span></span></a></sup> A W3C Community Group has been established in early 2020 in order to discuss and define such a resource.<sup about="#mwt437" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-css4_65-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;css4&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-css4-65&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-css4-65" id="mwB5o"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB5s"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB5w">[</span>65<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB50">]</span></span></a></sup> The actual kind of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_versioning" title="Software versioning" id="mwB54">versioning</a> is also up to debate, which means that the document, once produced, might not be called "CSS4".</p>

</section></section></section><section data-mw-section-id="38" id="mwB58" aria-labelledby="Browser_support"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Browser_support">Browser support</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Browser support"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwB6A">Each web browser uses a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_browser_engine" title="Web browser engine" class="mw-redirect" id="mwB6E">layout engine</a> to render web pages, and support for CSS functionality is not consistent between them. Because browsers do not parse CSS perfectly, multiple coding techniques have been developed to target specific browsers with workarounds (commonly known as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSS_hacks" title="CSS hacks" class="mw-redirect" id="mwB6I">CSS hacks</a> or CSS filters). The adoption of new functionality in CSS can be hindered by a lack of support in major browsers. For example, Internet Explorer was slow to add support for many CSS 3 features, which slowed the adoption of those features and damaged the browser's reputation among developers. Additionally, a proprietary syntax for the non-vendor-prefixed <code id="mwB6M">filter</code> property was used in some versions.<sup about="#mwt440" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-66" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-66&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-66" id="mwB6Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB6U"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB6Y">[</span>66<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB6c">]</span></span></a></sup> In order to ensure a consistent experience for their users, web developers often test their sites across multiple operating systems, browsers, and browser versions, increasing development time and complexity. Tools such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BrowserStack" title="BrowserStack" id="mwB6g">BrowserStack</a> have been built to reduce the complexity of maintaining these environments.</p>

<p id="mwB6k">In addition to these testing tools, many sites maintain lists of browser support for specific CSS properties, including <a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://caniuse.com/" class="external text" id="mwB6o">CanIUse</a> and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDN_Web_Docs" title="MDN Web Docs" id="mwB6s">MDN Web Docs</a>. Additionally, CSS 3 defines feature queries, which provide an <code id="mwB6w">@supports</code> directive that will allow developers to target browsers with support for certain functionality directly within their CSS.<sup about="#mwt443" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-67" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-67&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-67" id="mwB60"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB64"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB68">[</span>67<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB7A">]</span></span></a></sup> CSS that is not supported by older browsers can also sometimes be patched in using JavaScript <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polyfill_(programming)" title="Polyfill (programming)" id="mwB7E">polyfills</a>, which are pieces of JavaScript code designed to make browsers behave consistently. These workarounds—and the need to support fallback functionality—can add complexity to development projects, and consequently, companies frequently define a list of browser versions that they will and will not support.</p>

<p id="mwB7I">As websites adopt newer code standards that are incompatible with older browsers, these browsers can be cut off from accessing many of the resources on the web (sometimes intentionally).<sup about="#mwt446" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-68" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-68&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-68" id="mwB7M"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB7Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB7U">[</span>68<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB7Y">]</span></span></a></sup> However, many of the most popular sites on the internet are not just visually degraded on older browsers due to poor CSS support, but do not work at all, mostly due to the evolution of JavaScript and other web technologies.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="39" id="mwB7c" aria-labelledby="Limitations"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Limitations">Limitations</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Limitations"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwB7g">Some noted limitations of the current capabilities of CSS include:</p>

<section data-mw-section-id="40" id="mwB7k" aria-labelledby="Cannot_explicitly_declare_new_scope_independently_of_position"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Cannot_explicitly_declare_new_scope_independently_of_position">Cannot explicitly declare new scope independently of position</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Cannot explicitly declare new scope independently of position"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwB7o">Scoping rules for properties such as z-index look for the closest parent element with a <code id="mwB7s">position: absolute</code> or <code id="mwB7w">position: relative</code> declaration. This odd coupling has undesired effects. For example, it is impossible to avoid declaring a new scope when one is forced to adjust an element's position, preventing one from using the desired scope of a parent element.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="41" id="mwB70" aria-labelledby="Pseudo-class_dynamic_behavior_not_controllable"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Pseudo-class_dynamic_behavior_not_controllable">Pseudo-class dynamic behavior not controllable</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Pseudo-class dynamic behavior not controllable"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwB74">CSS implements pseudo-classes that allow a degree of user feedback by conditional application of alternative styles. One CSS pseudo-class, "<code class="mw-highlight mw-highlight-lang-css mw-content-ltr" style="" dir="ltr" typeof="mw:Extension/syntaxhighlight mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt447" id="mwB78" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;syntaxhighlight&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;css&quot;,&quot;class&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;style&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;inline&quot;:&quot;1&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;:hover&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;code&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Code&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;css&quot;},&quot;code&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;:hover&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><span class="p" id="mwB8A">:</span><span class="nd" id="mwB8E">hover</span></code>", is dynamic (equivalent of JavaScript "onmouseover") and has potential for misuse (e.g., implementing cursor-proximity popups),<sup about="#mwt451" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-69" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-69&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-69" id="mwB8I"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB8M"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB8Q">[</span>69<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB8U">]</span></span></a></sup> but CSS has no ability for a client to disable it (no "disable"-like property) or limit its effects (no "nochange"-like values for each property).</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="42" id="mwB8Y" aria-labelledby="Cannot_name_rules"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Cannot_name_rules">Cannot name rules</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: Cannot name rules"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwB8c">There is no way to name a CSS rule, which would allow (for example) client-side scripts to refer to the rule even if its selector changes.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="43" id="mwB8g" aria-labelledby="Cannot_include_styles_from_a_rule_into_another_rule"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Cannot_include_styles_from_a_rule_into_another_rule">Cannot include styles from a rule into another rule</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Cannot include styles from a rule into another rule"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwB8k">CSS styles often must be duplicated in several rules to achieve the desired effect, causing additional maintenance and requiring more thorough testing. Some new CSS features were proposed to solve this but were abandoned afterward.<sup about="#mwt454" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-70" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-70&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-70" id="mwB8o"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB8s"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB8w">[</span>70<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB80">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt457" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-71" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-71&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-71" id="mwB84"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB88"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB9A">[</span>71<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB9E">]</span></span></a></sup> Instead, authors may gain this ability by using more sophisticated stylesheet languages which compile to CSS, such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sass_(stylesheet_language)" title="Sass (stylesheet language)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwB9I">Sass</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Less_(stylesheet_language)" title="Less (stylesheet language)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwB9M">Less</a>, or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stylus_(stylesheet_language)" title="Stylus (stylesheet language)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwB9Q">Stylus</a>.</p>
</section><section data-mw-section-id="44" id="mwB9U" aria-labelledby="Cannot_target_specific_text_without_altering_markup"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Cannot_target_specific_text_without_altering_markup">Cannot target specific text without altering markup</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: Cannot target specific text without altering markup"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwB9Y">Besides the <code class="mw-highlight mw-highlight-lang-css mw-content-ltr" style="" dir="ltr" typeof="mw:Extension/syntaxhighlight mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt458" id="mwB9c" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;syntaxhighlight&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;css&quot;,&quot;class&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;style&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;inline&quot;:&quot;1&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;::first-letter&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;code&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Code&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;css&quot;},&quot;code&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;::first-letter&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><span class="p" id="mwB9g">::</span><span class="nd" id="mwB9k">first-letter</span></code> pseudo-element, one cannot target specific ranges of text without needing to utilize placeholder elements.</p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="45" id="mwB9o" aria-labelledby="Advantages"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Advantages">Advantages</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Advantages"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<section data-mw-section-id="46" id="mwB9s" aria-labelledby="Separation_of_content_from_presentation"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Separation_of_content_from_presentation">Separation of content from presentation</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=46" title="Edit section: Separation of content from presentation"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt460" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwB9w" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Main&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Main&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Separation of content and presentation&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt461" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt460" id="mwB90">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_content_and_presentation" title="Separation of content and presentation">Separation of content and presentation</a></div>
<p id="mwB94">CSS facilitates the publication of content in multiple presentation formats by adjusting styles based on various nominal parameters. These parameters include explicit user preferences (such as themes or font size), compatibility with different web browsers, the type of device used to view the content (e.g., desktop, tablet, or mobile device), screen resolutions, the geographic location of the user, and many other variables. CSS also enables responsive design, ensuring that content dynamically adapts to different screen sizes and orientations, enhancing accessibility and user experience across a wide range of environments.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="47" id="mwB98" aria-labelledby="Site-wide_consistency"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Site-wide_consistency">Site-wide consistency</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=47" title="Edit section: Site-wide consistency"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt462" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwB-A" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Main&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Main&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Style sheet (web development)&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt463" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt462" id="mwB-E">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Style_sheet_(web_development)" title="Style sheet (web development)">Style sheet (web development)</a></div>
<p id="mwB-I">When CSS is used effectively, in terms of inheritance and "cascading", a global style sheet can be used to affect and style elements site-wide. If the situation arises that the styling of the elements should be changed or adjusted, these changes can be made by editing rules in the global style sheet. Before CSS, this sort of maintenance was more difficult, expensive, and time-consuming.</p>
</section><section data-mw-section-id="48" id="mwB-M" aria-labelledby="Bandwidth"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Bandwidth">Bandwidth</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=48" title="Edit section: Bandwidth"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwB-Q">A stylesheet, internal or external, specifies the style once for a range of HTML elements selected by <code id="mwB-U">class</code>, type or relationship to others. This is much more efficient than repeating style information inline for each occurrence of the element. An external stylesheet is usually stored in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Browser_cache" title="Browser cache" class="mw-redirect" id="mwB-Y">browser cache</a>, and can therefore be used on multiple pages without being reloaded, further reducing data transfer over a network.</p>
</section><section data-mw-section-id="49" id="mwB-c" aria-labelledby="Page_reformatting"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Page_reformatting">Page reformatting</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=49" title="Edit section: Page reformatting"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt464" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwB-g" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Main&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Main&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Progressive enhancement&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt465" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt464" id="mwB-k">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_enhancement" title="Progressive enhancement">Progressive enhancement</a></div>
<p id="mwB-o">With a simple change of one line, a different style sheet can be used for the same page. This has advantages for accessibility, as well as providing the ability to tailor a page or site to different target devices. Furthermore, devices not able to understand the styling  (such as older browsers) can still display the content.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="50" id="mwB-s" aria-labelledby="Accessibility"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Accessibility">Accessibility</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=50" title="Edit section: Accessibility"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt466" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwB-w" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Main&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Main&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Tableless web design#Accessibility&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt467" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt466" id="mwB-0">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tableless_web_design#Accessibility" title="Tableless web design">Tableless web design §<span typeof="mw:Entity">&nbsp;</span>Accessibility</a></div>
<p id="mwB-4">Without CSS, web designers must typically lay out their pages with techniques such as HTML tables that hinder accessibility for vision-impaired users (see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tableless_web_design#Accessibility" title="Tableless web design" about="#mwt468" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwB-8" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Section link&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Section_link&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Tableless web design&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Accessibility&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}">Tableless web design §<span typeof="mw:Entity">&nbsp;</span>Accessibility</a>).</p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="51" id="mwB_A" aria-labelledby="Standardization"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Standardization">Standardization</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=51" title="Edit section: Standardization"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<section data-mw-section-id="52" id="mwB_E" aria-labelledby="Frameworks"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Frameworks">Frameworks</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=52" title="Edit section: Frameworks"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt469" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwB_I" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Main&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Main&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;CSS framework&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt470" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Hatnote/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;}}"></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt469" id="mwB_M">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSS_framework" title="CSS framework">CSS framework</a></div>
<p id="mwB_Q"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSS_framework" title="CSS framework" id="mwB_U">CSS frameworks</a> are prepared <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Library_(computing)" title="Library (computing)" id="mwB_Y">libraries</a> that are meant to allow for easier, more standards-compliant styling of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_page" title="Web page" id="mwB_c">web pages</a> using the Cascading Style Sheets language. CSS frameworks include <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blueprint_(CSS_framework)" title="Blueprint (CSS framework)" id="mwB_g">Blueprint</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bootstrap_(front-end_framework)" title="Bootstrap (front-end framework)" id="mwB_k">Bootstrap</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foundation_(framework)" title="Foundation (framework)" id="mwB_o">Foundation</a> and Materialize. Like programming and scripting language libraries, CSS frameworks are usually incorporated as external .css sheets referenced in the HTML <code class="mw-highlight mw-highlight-lang-html mw-content-ltr" style="" dir="ltr" typeof="mw:Extension/syntaxhighlight mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt471" id="mwB_s" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;syntaxhighlight&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;html&quot;,&quot;class&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;style&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;inline&quot;:&quot;1&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&lt;head&gt;&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;code&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Code&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;html&quot;},&quot;code&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;&lt;head&gt;&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><span class="p" id="mwB_w">&lt;</span><span class="nt" id="mwB_0">head</span><span class="p" id="mwB_4">&gt;</span></code>. They provide a number of ready-made options for designing and laying out the web page. Although many of these frameworks have been published, some authors use them mostly for rapid prototyping, or for learning from, and prefer to 'handcraft' CSS that is appropriate to each published site without the design, maintenance and download overhead of having many unused features in the site's styling.<sup about="#mwt475" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-72" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-72&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-72" id="mwB_8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwCAA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwCAE">[</span>72<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwCAI">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="53" id="mwCAM" aria-labelledby="Design_methodologies"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Design_methodologies">Design methodologies</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=53" title="Edit section: Design methodologies"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwCAQ">As the size of CSS resources used in a project increases, a development team often needs to decide on a common design methodology to keep them organized. The goals are ease of development, ease of collaboration during development, and performance of the deployed stylesheets in the browser. Popular methodologies include OOCSS (object-oriented CSS), ACSS (atomic CSS), CSS (organic Cascade Style Sheet), SMACSS (scalable and modular architecture for CSS), and BEM (block, element, modifier).<sup about="#mwt478" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-73" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-73&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-73" id="mwCAU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwCAY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwCAc">[</span>73<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwCAg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="54" id="mwCAk" aria-labelledby="See_also"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="See_also">See also</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=CSS&amp;action=edit&amp;section=54" title="Edit section: See also"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<li id="mwCA0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSS-in-JS" title="CSS-in-JS" id="mwCA4">CSS-in-JS</a></li></ul>
